## E-PMTA-71 — Legacy VMTA push sequence — 2026-10-25

Read-only DMS `PmtaController::push_vmta` and `Console\Commands\pmta\PmtaVmta::push` show the legacy VMTA path writes `/etc/pmta/conf/tmp_vmta_<user>`, validates it with `pmta reload`, then appends it to `vmta_<user>`, reloads again, and removes the temporary file. ## E-AFF-07 — NewHitPath selected-offer import — 2026-10-24

Read-only DMS `SponsorsManager/NewHitPath.php::get_offer/get_assets/get_creatives`, shared `Network` rules and `OfferAction::add_auto` establish the selected offer flow: bearer-authenticated `/api/campaigns/{id}` request; skip offers without a redirect; optionally fetch `/emails` for From/Subject when `email_count > 0`; derive vertical from the offer name, country codes from the name plus `geo.from`, type from name/description, and allowed days from description; append a slash to the first redirect URL; use `optout` and description; fetch email bodies for creative processing; deduplicate raw bodies before rewriting; and save the offer inactive with sender/HTML/image assets. DMS uses `withoutVerifying()` and sends the bearer token to each image URL.

AlphaLink implements the selected metadata, exact raw-duplicate-before-whitespace-normalization order for sender lines, DMS-derived rules, HTML encoding normalization, source-order deduplication, redirect/unsubscribe token replacement and workspace-scoped image assets. Provider requests use TLS verification, public DNS pinning and no redirects. Image fetches are limited to 2 MiB and supported JPEG/PNG/GIF MIME types; the API token is sent only to the configured provider host, never a third-party image origin. These protections may reject self-signed provider certificates, HTTP/private image hosts, oversized/unsupported images, or CDN images requiring the API token; those differences remain unverified and are not accepted as provider parity.

The live AlphaLink Add Offer page showed NewHitPath as supported but had no saved Sponsor to select. The **38-test / 395-assertion** focused affiliate/creative/Offer/Sponsor regression passed with synthetic provider responses and DNS only; Pint passed. No affiliate credentials or DMS rows were read, no provider API was called, and no DMS state changed. HasOffers was implemented afterward in E-AFF-08. NewHitPath live behavior, provider login/session, refresh, extension sharing and conversion reconciliation remain open; DMS-AFF-001 is not accepted.

## E-AFF-04 — EverFlow creative import and public email assets — 2026-10-24

## E-SRV-LIVE-20 — Single Install private interface IP matches DMS — 2026-10-23

Compared the user's Install screenshot and the saved OVH server against read-only DMS source. DMS `Centos9::get_ips()` runs `ip -o addr show scope global`, so it lists host interface addresses, including RFC1918 addresses. `ServerAction::get_server_installable_ips()` only moves the registered server IP to the front when it is present in that list; the Install controller marks MAIN only on exact IP equality. During owned-domain DNS setup, `ServerInstall::setup_records()` detects private/reserved addresses and calls `Centos9::get_public_ip()`, which runs `curl -s --interface <interface-ip> ifconfig.me`. The PMTA VMTA continues to use the local interface address as `smtp-source-ip`.

On the user's saved uninstalled OVH host, the real AlphaLink 8766 Load IPs view showed the DMS-equivalent local interface `10.2.3.39`; its registered provider/public IP is `57.129.92.64` and is not directly assigned to a host interface, so MAIN remains false exactly as in DMS. A read-only SSH call through AlphaLink's fingerprint-verified installer session ran the same DMS public-IP lookup for `10.2.3.39`; it returned `57.129.92.64`. Thus the private row is the address PMTA can bind, and DMS/AlphaLink resolve the actual public address when writing owned-domain DNS. No install, DNS-provider write, interface configuration, or other host mutation ran. Focused AlphaLink tests for private-to-public DNS resolution and local VMTA IP behavior pass (**16 tests / 83 assertions**). The screenshot is not a DMS parity defect; full install acceptance remains separate.

The correct installer payload is the user-copied DMS `storage/app/install` bundle in AlphaLink's private persistent volume. Linux Mint 22.3 is Debian/Ubuntu based and this workstation has neither `rpm` nor `dnf`; it is not a valid target for the DMS AlmaLinux RPM path. The GitHub Docker example supplied by the user describes PMTA 4.5 on CentOS 7, requires a license-associated MAC address, and does not establish AlmaLinux 9 support.

The authenticated AlphaLink browser page defaulted to PMTA 4.0 after reload. Target validation is version-specific: 4.5 passes all 11 checks; 4.0 and 5.0 remain unverified and correctly fail the package/target checks. A fresh real target check through AlphaLink's saved host-key-verified SSH session parsed the 4.5 RPM metadata, returned `digests OK`, and resolved a DNF plan (59 packages). `dnf --assumeno` exited 1 because it intentionally aborted; it installed nothing. This did not prove a vendor signature or contractual license entitlement.

The first actual run reached `Setup Services` and failed because installer SSH commands inherited an 8-second timeout; phpseclib left the exec channel open. AlphaLink now uses a 300-second installer timeout, disconnects on a failed command, and throws instead of treating `false` as an empty successful response. Regression checks passed: PHP 233/1,425, Node 7/7, Pint, and `git diff --check`.

The second run went through the real AlphaLink controller, process-pool worker, saved credential and real server (not a fake transport). The application transcript reports success through host preparation, PMTA 4.5, VMTAs and firewall. Independent post-install SSH checks confirmed RPM `PowerMTA-4.5r11-201709182152.x86_64`, exact DMS 4.5 daemon-patch hash at `/usr/sbin/pmtad`, exact DMS license-file hash at `/etc/pmta/license`, `pmta` and `pmtahttp` active, PMTA CLI/status responding, and listeners on ports 25, 1212 and 1213. AlphaLink marked the server installed; the browser Servers page visibly shows `INSTALLED: YES`. Optional tracking, proxy, IMAP, SPF, DKIM, DMARC and HTTPS remained off. DMS state was not changed, and no list or affiliate data was copied.

The DMS host-preparation commands set the hostname to the selected domain, changed resolver nameservers, removed old HTTP/mail packages, and left SELinux permissive with its config set disabled. These are material DMS side effects on the test server. Contractual license scope remains outside what the runtime can prove; the PMTA daemon did accept the exact DMS license file and is running. Overall DMS parity remains `TESTING`, not accepted: this establishes a successful base PMTA install, not every optional feature or complete visual/behavioral parity.

Current checkpoint: PMTA 4.5r11 is installed and running on the saved AlmaLinux 9.8 x86_64 server through AlphaLink's real workflow. The installation loop is no longer blocked by the old PMTA 4.0 default or the 8-second SSH timeout. Remaining work is to close the other version evidence, compare the populated server/log UI against DMS, and implement/test the remaining DMS workflows.

## Current implementation slice

DMS-SRV-001 (server registration) and DMS-SRV-002 (server list/returned rows) remain `TESTING`, not accepted. AlphaLink has a provider-backed DMS-shaped registration form, multi-IP API flow, encrypted credential storage, expiry and separate Test Connection UI, plus a workspace-scoped list, returned-row filter and DMS-style Add modal. The local IP guard blocks private, reserved and documentation ranges before socket creation. A synthetic local registration was displayed and removed after verification.

DMS-SRV-003 (Allowed Users, lock/status, return and restore) is `IMPLEMENTING`. Source evidence E-SRV-02 maps DMS mailers to workspace operators and DMS managers to workspace owners/admins. The local backend applies same-workspace operator assignments, filters operators to assigned servers (including returned rows), permits assigned operators to change status or return unlocked servers, and limits assignment/lock/restore to owners/admins. Returning soft-deletes the row; locked servers cannot be returned. Individual and bulk permission forms match the DMS interaction, and transitions emit audit events. Tests verify cross-workspace rejection and state changes. Populated visual comparison of the controls remains open; production DMS rows stay hidden by the relay.

DMS-SRV-004 (server health history) is `IMPLEMENTING`. E-SRV-03 traces DMS's every-ten-minute job: active installed servers receive concurrent SSH, Apache/port 80 and PMTA/configured-port checks; the job stores down flags with timestamps and fills missing country by Pro IP-API with an end-of-day cache and MaxMind city fallback. DMS history shows ID, three service states and time; records are pruned after three months. AlphaLink now schedules concurrent raw-TCP port checks, persists workspace-scoped history, shows latest SSH status/history, and prunes after three months. These checks use no SSH login or fingerprint prompt. PMTA defaults to 1212 and is configurable. The same API country contract is implemented; MaxMind fallback needs GeoIP2 and a database, and the target does not currently include GeoIP2. Focused tests pass (6/27), and the latest full suite passes (41/422). Tests fake HTTP and use loopback; no external server or geo API was contacted.

The owner confirmed that DMS does not request an SSH fingerprint during Test Connection or installation. AlphaLink no longer displays or accepts fingerprint input: Test Connection logs in directly over SSH/SFTP, and preflight automatically pins the first host key internally so later key changes are rejected without an operator prompt. DMS live SSH/install actions remain untested and prohibited. The installation execution route remains disabled.

DMS-INST-001 bulk installation remains `IMPLEMENTING`: the DMS-shaped page now matches source-backed eligibility, options, APPLY-to-all (including applying the blank selection), owned-domain guard and row-removal confirmation. The submission builder drops rows with no domain and sends the DMS-shaped fields; a backend contract validator checks the nonempty row list, numeric key, PMTA 40/45/50 and seven booleans, reducing rows to server/domain IDs. An injected fake-runner coordinator verifies DMS's row-level exception logging and parent-success semantics. `DmsBulkInstallSubdomainGenerator` now carries the exact 1,022-entry DMS word pool and Laravel random selection; a SHA-256 test checks its ordered contents. `DmsBulkInstallIpPlanner` reproduces IPv4-before-IPv6 ordering, packed binary sorting, duplicate removal, primary-IP flag and first/primary alias rules. Four synthetic IP tests compare formatted rows, exercise generated aliases and feed output through DNS/VMTA planners. `DmsBulkInstallDnsRecordPlanner` reproduces guarded DNS record shapes/order, SPF/DKIM/DMARC options and private-IP fallback; four synthetic tests cover those results. `DmsBulkInstallVmtaPlanner` renders DMS's per-IP/per-ISP VMTAs, IPv6 naming, optional domain-key lines, owned-domain inbound blocks and CentOS 9 feedback loop; three synthetic tests compare exact config strings and branch differences. All planners are offline and have no provider/PMTA adapter. Read-only remote address discovery is implemented (E-INST-15) but not wired to the runner; E-INST-23 adds a workspace ISP catalog and passes its lowercased, first-seen unique names—including inactive ISPs—into row preparation; production ISP values remain empty. The usual Cloud page selects non-owned domains, so its DNS write branch is a no-op. The coordinator remains sequential; no production submission/run-start route or remote runner is registered. E-INST-12 now persists synthetic-run transcripts and exposes the workspace-scoped GET read endpoint, but no production run feeds it and no browser polling/log UI exists. E-INST-13 maps the CentOS 7/9 PMTA asset/option branches offline; artifact validation and all remote execution remain absent. INSTALL remains disabled. DMS source shows SSH/SFTP login, x86_64 and supported OS checks, IP discovery, host/DNS/service changes, PMTA/VMTA/firewall setup and installation-state writes. No fingerprint prompt appears in that source flow. Username/provider mappings, populated-page comparison, package/license validation, rollback, remote progress and full topology remain open. No installation ran and no DMS host or state was changed; AlphaLink target access was limited to the previously recorded read-only preflight probe.

The next completed offline slice maps DMS `finish_install()` finalization: delete old server MTAs/IPs, insert the formatted IP rows with owned-domain DNS rules, conditionally mark an owned domain `used`, save install parameters, and mark the server installed last. `DmsBulkInstallCompletionPlanner` plus three synthetic tests model this operation order and reject malformed row/options input. ## 90% reconstruction goal

The requested 90% means source-evidenced frontend and backend workflows with working local implementations, executed tests, browser parity checks, and all material differences resolved or explicitly approved. The feature inventory is incomplete, workflow weights are not yet defined, and no feature is `PARITY_CONFIRMED`; therefore no honest percentage is available. Do not count screens, routes, documents, or code files as completed coverage. Continue with read-only reference evidence and one dependency-ready vertical slice at a time.

## Campaign Test relay denial diagnosis — 2026-10-24

Read-only inspection of the selected installed server confirmed PowerMTA 4.5r11 on the saved workspace listener at port 1212. The active PMTA config includes `/etc/pmta/settings/*`; the access file exists, but no active source block contains `always-allow-relaying`. The workspace DEV IPS and PRODUCTION IPS lists are empty, and the failed SMTP source address does not appear in any configured source rule. DMS CentOS install source builds the access file from DEV IPS and expands its `pmta_source` template there. No PMTA config write/reload, reinstall, email retry, mailbox access, DNS operation, or DMS mutation occurred. The minimal repair is a single-source PMTA SMTP relay rule plus `pmta reload`; it expands relay access to the AlphaLink app host and remains pending action-time confirmation. E-CAMP-63 records the sanitized trace. Campaign Test and DMS sender parity remain TESTING.

## E-TRACK-06 — Callback install-key resolution and dashboard data foundation — 2026-10-26

The DMS `RedirectHandlerController::stats` and `stats_drop` source authenticates callbacks with the shared mini-app key installed on the sending server. AlphaLink's endpoints previously required only a separate `ALPHALINK_TRACKING_KEY`, so callbacks from an AlphaLink-installed server could be rejected even when its saved workspace install key matched. `TrackingIngestAuthenticator` now accepts the explicit tracking-key override and the effective install key: the saved workspace value takes precedence, the configured install value is used only when no saved value exists, and a saved empty value remains fail-closed. Both `/api/v1/stats` and `/api/v1/stats-drop` use this resolver.

Focused running-container regression: Campaign Tracking, Drop Tracking, Dashboard and List History suites pass **23 tests / 196 assertions**; targeted Pint passes. No DMS action, email, provider request or saved local event ran. Sender token generation/callback wiring and connected event population remain open; keep Tracking and Dashboard TESTING. ## E-PUB-01 — Public subscriber, contact, and opt-out source behavior — 2026-10-26

Read-only DMS source maps three public endpoints, each throttled to two requests per minute. Subscribe validates email/IP/user-agent and optional referer, then stores the email and request context. Opt-out exact-matches an email; a hit queues the `opt_out` type change and activity log, preserves `lead`, and does not move the address to a country List. A miss makes no state/log change. Both paths return HTTP 204. AlphaLink has no matching public routes; its authenticated contact API and campaign `unsubscribe` tracking are separate workflows. This missing opt-out input also leaves the List History opt-out count unavailable. No DMS request or recipient lookup occurred.

## E-DB-05 — Contact versus List-specific import attributes — 2026-10-27

Current source inspection finds `Contact.profile` is a single encrypted JSON field on the workspace contact; `ContactListMembership` stores List/type/status/timestamps but no profile fields. ## E-DEPLOY-01 — Release-based Ubuntu installation and redeploy path — 2026-10-28

The single Ubuntu installer now keeps a protected Git checkout under `/var/www/alphalink/source`, exports immutable full-commit releases under `/var/www/alphalink/releases` without Git metadata, keeps Laravel storage shared across releases, and activates code through an atomic `current` symlink. Its `--deploy [BRANCH]` mode builds dependencies before downtime, prompts for a private GitHub token without persisting it, enters maintenance mode, pauses Horizon/suppression/scheduler work, creates and checks a PostgreSQL dump, applies migrations, checks HTTPS health, then resumes queues and scheduling and verifies Horizon. Failed migrations or health checks trigger a full PostgreSQL database recreate from the pre-deploy dump and a switch back to the previous release; incomplete recovery leaves maintenance mode active. Successful deployments retain the newest five releases. PostgreSQL remains the required database.

`bash -n`, installer help/plan/deploy-plan modes, invalid-argument rejection, worker-sizing cases, and `git diff --check` pass. A disposable PostgreSQL 18.6 recovery drill verified archive listing, full database recreation, restore of pre-deploy synthetic data, and removal of a migration-only table. No saved AlphaLink database, GitHub push, Ubuntu 26.04 install, live deploy/rollback, off-host backup, valid 100 MB list import, or 700 MB suppression upload was run. This is implementation and synthetic recovery evidence, not production acceptance. Configure and test off-host backup/restore, then perform a fresh Ubuntu 26.04 install and a controlled deploy/rollback drill before enabling production traffic.

## E-TRACK-25 — Campaign callback queue-retry idempotency — 2026-10-28

The campaign tracking endpoint now creates a UUID for each accepted HTTP callback and carries it into the retryable queue job. The processor uses that UUID as the immutable engagement-event ID and returns the existing event when the same job is redelivered after commit. A mismatched payload with a reused request ID fails closed. Separate HTTP callbacks receive separate UUIDs, so legitimate repeat opens/clicks remain distinct. Existing membership and offer-suppression writes stay in the same transaction as the event.

`CampaignTrackingTest` passes **27 tests / 186 assertions** on SQLite `:memory:` and disposable PostgreSQL 18.6; the new regression handles the same queued job twice, verifies one event and one membership transition, then handles a separate callback and verifies a second event. Targeted Pint and `git diff --check` pass. This adds AlphaLink queue retry safety without claiming the DMS sender's unknown retry identity. Concurrent duplicate-job contention, a real Horizon acknowledgement failure, saved-workspace behavior, browser parity, and native sender callback wiring remain unverified. The saved PostgreSQL cluster, the separate 5433 database, DMS, PMTA, and email were untouched. Keep DMS-TRACK-003 TESTING.

## E-DB-18 — Bounded campaign recipient claims and suppression recheck — 2026-10-28

Implemented `ClaimCampaignSendBatch` as a transactional pre-transport stage over the E-DB-17 send ledger. It locks the run, uses bounded snapshot-position paging from the saved `start_from`, fingerprints claim request inputs for idempotent replay, and records address-free claims. Before the first batch it streams and verifies the saved suppression artifact and freshness; every batch then requires the live offer digest to remain equal to the immutable snapshot. Each batch rechecks active contact state, workspace/offer suppression events, and offer-list exclusions against frozen selected-source provenance. A deduplicated recipient stays eligible if any selected contributing List is not suppressed.

`CampaignSendExecutionLedgerTest` passes **10 tests / 62 assertions** on SQLite `:memory:` and **10 / 62** on disposable PostgreSQL 18.6. Regression cases cover suppression-file tampering, a suppression refresh between batches, workspace and offer events, inactive contacts, multi-List source suppression, start position, idempotent replay/conflict, database scoping, and preserving send history on migration rollback. Targeted Pint passed in the PHP 8.3 app container. The first PostgreSQL run exposed a broken fixture that expected two contacts but built one; the fixture was corrected before the passing SQLite and PostgreSQL runs. The temporary PostgreSQL container was removed and the unrelated 5433 project database was untouched.

This remains a claim foundation, not sending. Consent/channel policy, final transport-time recheck, queue dispatch, lease recovery, pause/cancel, attempt outcome transitions, uncertain acceptance handling, launch/UI lifecycle, browser parity, and native DMS sender outcome parity remain open. No email, PMTA operation, DMS runtime action, or saved workspace data was used. Keep SEND UNAVAILABLE and DMS-CAMP-001 IMPLEMENTING.

## E-DEPLOY-03 — Release storage-link permissions — 2026-10-28

The fresh installer already creates Laravel's public-storage link as root. The redeploy path created it as `www-data` after making each immutable release root-owned and mode `0750`; Laravel's `storage:link` command writes under the release's `public` directory, so the command would fail with a permission error. Redeploy now runs only this symlink command as root; migrations, optimization and application runtime remain `www-data`. Confirmed the effective directory permissions in a disposable container probe: `www-data` cannot write the release directory, while root can create the link. Installer `bash -n`, help/plan/deploy-plan, target worker sizing, `git diff --check`, and both default/custom-worker Horizon test runs pass. A clean Ubuntu 26 install and real redeploy/rollback remain unverified.

## E-DB-20 — Expired campaign batch lease recovery — 2026-10-28

Implemented `ReclaimExpiredCampaignSendBatch`: expired leases can be reassigned only for recipients without a started transport attempt. Replaying a started attempt's idempotency key now fails closed instead of authorizing a second transport submission. ## E-DB-23 — Time-leading analytics source indexes — 2026-10-28

The daily analytics rebuild filters campaign events, event provenance, lead conversions, Drop visitors, and PMTA feedback by date across workspaces. Their existing indexes began with workspace or campaign, so the global date windows could not seek directly to the requested period. Added time-leading `(event_time, workspace_id)` indexes for all five source tables. PostgreSQL builds these concurrently outside a migration transaction and removes an invalid concurrent-build remnant before retry; SQLite uses regular schema indexes.

Campaign event provenance is written in the tracking transaction with `campaign_engagement_event_sources.created_at` copied from its parent event's `created_at`. The list-attribution subquery retains the parent-event date/action checks and also filters the matching provenance timestamp, allowing its new index to bound that scan without changing the event-date rule. A tracking regression verifies timestamp equality.

A disposable PostgreSQL 18.6 probe used 500,000 synthetic campaign events and 880,000 provenance rows with matching event/source timestamps. For the one-day attribution query, with the event-time index already present but no provenance-time index, PostgreSQL sequentially scanned all provenance rows; execution took 131 ms with 9,287 shared buffer hits. After adding the provenance-time index, it bitmap-scanned 30,502 provenance rows and the 15,251 matching events; execution took 52 ms with 552 buffer hits and 31 reads. This is one warm synthetic query-plan comparison, not a production throughput or 10M-record claim.

The focused analytics, tracking, and PMTA-feedback suites pass **58 tests / 660 assertions** on SQLite `:memory:` and the same **58 / 660** on disposable PostgreSQL 18.6. The index-column assertions and event/provenance timestamp invariant pass on both engines. The migration rolled back and reapplied on disposable PostgreSQL. Targeted Pint, PHP syntax, and whitespace checks pass. Saved-app browser remains blocked by PostgreSQL 16 at 127.0.0.1:5432 being stopped (E-TOOLS-10). Production migration I/O/disk cost, full rebuild memory/throughput, PostgreSQL contention, 10M behavior, and browser parity remain open. No saved AlphaLink database, DMS data/action, email, or PMTA host was used. Keep DMS-TRACK-003, DMS-CAMP-007, and Dashboard rows TESTING.

## E-CAMP-110 — Avoid duplicate full-size suppression artifact — 2026-10-28

`DmsOfferSuppressionFile::normalizeDownloadedFile` streamed the sorted canonical result into a system temporary file and then copied the complete file into private app storage. The normalizer now writes the canonical rows directly to a unique temporary file under the local private-storage root, computes SHA-256 while streaming, and moves that file to its digest-addressed path on the same filesystem. The installed `league/flysystem-local` 3.35.3 adapter uses `rename()` for this move. This removes one full-size output copy from the upload/download normalization path; the input and final normalized artifact still need storage, and PostgreSQL temporary deduplication/sorting can require substantial additional disk.

`DmsOfferManagementTest` passes **17 tests / 155 assertions** on SQLite `:memory:` in the PHP 8.3 app container. The regression verifies 1,002 unique entries, deduplication across 500-row database batches, an email split across a 64 KiB read boundary, the canonical digest, and no staging artifact after upload. A forced digest-path publication failure also removes the staged file and leaves offer metadata unchanged. Targeted Pint, PHP syntax, and `git diff --check` pass. No 700 MB transfer, Ubuntu 26/PHP-FPM/Nginx request, PostgreSQL temp-table scale, target disk-headroom, parallel worker, or 10M throughput test ran. No saved offer, provider, production server, email, DMS data, or DMS action was used. DMS-DROP-003 remains TESTING.

## E-DB-25 — Encrypted point-in-time campaign recipient addresses — 2026-10-28

DMS's audited campaign path creates a file-backed point-in-time recipient set and returns the recipient file to the native sender (`audit/DMS_REA_SENDER_RESOLUTION_2026-10-22.md`, `audit/ALPHALINK_DATABASE_BLUEPRINT_DMS_TRACE.md`). AlphaLink previously persisted only contact IDs and keyed email hashes, so a later contact edit could prevent the immutable snapshot from reproducing its original address. AlphaLink now stores each snapshot address in a separate append-only table encrypted with Laravel's application key; snapshot revision copies carry the encrypted value forward. Addresses remain hidden from model serialization and are absent from claims and queue payloads. The send planner refuses a missing address or one whose keyed identity differs from the snapshot.

`BackfillCampaignSnapshotAddresses` restores a legacy address only when the current contact's normalized email recomputes both the stored contact HMAC and the snapshot HMAC. If the address changed or the lookup key is unavailable, the command reports the row unresolved and the planner keeps that legacy snapshot unsendable; the operator must create a fresh snapshot. Preserve the application key for backups and restores. The migration refuses rollback after encrypted addresses exist.

`CampaignSendExecutionLedgerTest` plus `DmsCampaignSendCatalogTest` pass **45 tests / 971 assertions** on SQLite `:memory:`. Snapshot creation now verifies each normalized address against the configured contact HMAC before recording it; a tampered identity fails closed. The ledger suite passes **20 / 147** on disposable PostgreSQL 18.6; the 1,002-recipient snapshot creation/address-copy test passes **1 / 12** on PostgreSQL 18.6. Targeted Pint and `git diff --check` pass. These tests use synthetic addresses; no email was sent and no saved database or DMS action was used. Address storage cost at 10M recipients, key rotation, real Ubuntu migration, browser parity, sender worker, native launch/history UI and DMS delivery parity remain unverified. Keep DMS-CAMP-001 IMPLEMENTING and SEND UNAVAILABLE.

## E-SPO-10 — Sponsor page resilience on fresh releases — 2026-10-28

The Sponsor and My Sponsors controllers derived the browser-helper extension ID from a manifest stored only under ignored runtime storage. A fresh release without that optional asset could throw while rendering either page. AlphaLink now renders both pages without the helper package, displays an unavailable notice, and omits the download action. A regression test mocks the missing package and confirms both routes return HTTP 200. Sponsor management and login tests pass 22 tests / 263 assertions; Pint and syntax checks pass. Anonymous production GETs redirect to sign-in; SSH read-only access was denied, so the authenticated production 500 was not independently confirmed. Extension download/handoff still requires staging the helper bundle. This fixes the crash path, not provider parity.

## E-INST-84 — Fresh-install PMTA relay settings and release preflight — 2026-10-28

Fresh Ubuntu installation now auto-detects a private bundle uploaded to `/root/install-assets`, validates that all PMTA 4.0/4.5/5.0 and selected-service assets exist before changing the host, and stages them with owner-only permissions. The first-owner bootstrap now collects AlphaLink's stable public IPv4 egress address(es) and saves them in the new workspace's encrypted PMTA RELAY SOURCE IPS setting. Installers can explicitly choose app-only mode without the asset bundle; PMTA install remains blocked until assets are staged. Deploy and interrupted-resume paths preflight Laravel route serialization before switching releases or entering downtime. `--deploy` reuses a complete inactive release from a previous attempt, preserves incomplete collisions in a quarantine directory, and health-checks the already-active commit as a no-op.

`BootstrapOwnerTest` passes **2 tests / 10 assertions**; PMTA deployment tests pass **12 / 55**; installer-settings/runtime tests pass **16 / 129**; sponsor management/login tests pass **22 / 257** with the private archive test skipped because its optional bundle is not staged. The full PHP suite exits successfully: **889 tests / 8,244 assertions**, with **1 skipped test and 12 PHPUnit notices**. Vite build, Laravel route-cache smoke, shell syntax/help/plan checks, public-IPv4 validation cases, complete/missing-asset layout fixtures, targeted Pint, and `git diff --check` pass. No fresh Ubuntu server, PMTA host install, relay SMTP test, or external DNS action was run. The operator must supply the true stable egress address; the installer does not infer or promise it from the server's inbound address. Keep server deployment TESTING until a clean Ubuntu 26.04 install and post-install PMTA readiness checks pass.

## E-INST-86 — Production Single/Bulk install worker startup diagnosis — 2026-10-28

Read-only inspection of the live AlphaLink application host found that the Single Install POST at 18:48:46 UTC and Bulk Install POST at 18:55:22 UTC each returned HTTP 202, but their run records stayed `queued`, transcripts remained empty, and no `infrastructure:execute-run` worker was present. The launcher used PHP `PHP_BINARY`, which in this host’s PHP-FPM process resolves to `/usr/sbin/php-fpm8.5`; the actual CLI is `/usr/bin/php`. A read-only `artisan list` under `www-data` succeeds, so this is separate from the previously corrected Composer permissions issue. `DmsInfrastructureRunLauncher` now invokes the executable at `PHP_BINDIR/php` and fails clearly if it is unavailable. PHP syntax and a local CLI-path executable smoke check pass; the focused PHPUnit test could not run because this workspace lacks `ext-bcmath`, `ext-dom`, and `ext-xml` and has no installed `vendor/` tree. No target SSH or install command ran; the two queued records were left unchanged. Production deployment, focused test execution, and a controlled install run remain pending. Keep DMS-INST-004/005 TESTING.
